*) The first or Junior Grade <term>Juniorate: the first level of initiation for newcomers to the order.</term> is already printed in the well-known work: *The Rosicrucian in his Nakedness* <note>original: "Der Rosenkreuzer in seiner Blöße" (1781), a famous exposé by Hans Heinrich von Ecker und Eckhoffen that revealed the secret rituals of the first Rosicrucian grade.</note>.

# Preliminary Note.

The following writing, along with its preface, came into my hands by chance during a journey to S. last year. I found it so remarkable <note>The German "merkwürdig" here carries its older meaning of "worthy of note" rather than just "strange."</note> that I cannot refrain from presenting it to the unbiased eyes of the public. I cannot be accused of any betrayal, as I am a profane outsider <term>profane; from the Latin 'profanus,' referring to someone who stands "outside the temple" and is not initiated into secret rites</term> regarding all possible mysteries, and I entered into no obligations or oaths when obtaining this manuscript. My

> If anyone should complain that this is more biting than is fitting for a Christian—it was not I, but Democritus who said it. <note>original: "Si quis calumnietur mordacius esse quam deceat Christianum — non ego, sed Democritus dixit." This quote is from the preface of Desiderius Erasmus’s *The Praise of Folly* (1511). What then are these <term>Rosicrucians</term><note>A secret brotherhood that claimed to possess ancient esoteric wisdom and the power to transform both lead into gold and the human spirit into a higher state.</note>? asked Shah Gebal—of the philosopher <term>Danishmende</term><note>A name of Persian origin meaning "learned" or "wise man."</note> who was sitting before him.

Danishmende: They are <term>exorcists</term><note>original: "Teufelsbanner," literally "devil-banishers."</note> and <term>alchemists</term><note>original: "Goldmacher," literally "gold-makers."</note>, Your Majesty.

"Then do tell us something about them," interrupted the curious, dark-eyed <term>Nurmahal</term><note>A name meaning "Light of the Palace."</note>.

**Danish.**<note>An abbreviation for the philosopher Danishmende.</note> Exactly so: The <term>Rosicrucians</term> are one part of those men who unite in secret for various purposes, and these occupy themselves with these high, secret sciences. — Always and at all times there have been such mysteries and associations.

brutality of the customs of their century, and to transform the peoples into civilized nations. This was the purpose of an Osiris in Egypt, an Orpheus in Thrace, a Zoroaster in Persia, a Minos in Crete, Ciniras in Cyprus, and Erechtheus in Athens: such <term>Mysteries</term><note>Secret religious rites of the ancient world, such as the Eleusinian Mysteries, which involved initiation and promised spiritual insight.</note> were established only for the spreading of the knowledge of a single God, and for the attainment of the moral virtue that alone makes mankind happy.

"Good, dear Doctor," said the yawning Shah Gebal—"but to the point, if I may ask—"

It was one of the happiest thoughts of the ancients to make use of mankind’s inclination for the wondrous in order to lead them to wisdom and virtue: for this reason, they also established certain stages and ceremonies which no one who wished to be initiated could avoid: this

"Knights Templar? Knights Templar?" asked the
curious Nurmahal. —

"Yes, my fair lady—these people were the more enlightened portion among those ecstatic fanatics <note>The author uses 'fanatics' to describe the Crusaders, reflecting an Enlightenment-era skepticism toward religious wars.</note> who bound themselves together to snatch a piece of sandy desert, which concealed the tomb of their prophet <note>referring to Jesus Christ</note>, from the hands of its owners out of holy religious zeal. A poor dervish <note>A reference to Peter the Hermit (c. 1050–1115), whose fiery preaching helped ignite the First Crusade.</note> who, instead of preaching humility and peace, instilled this idea of conquest and encouraged the nations of Europe to murder, was to blame for the deaths of 2,350,000 <note>This high number reflects contemporary 18th-century estimates of Crusade casualties rather than modern census data.</note> unfortunate souls who met their end in the sandy deserts of Palestine. Their prophet would not—or could not—support the rescuers of his tomb in their 'glorious' intention, and so this holy purpose was thwarted.

"Despite all my devotion to the honor of my own Prophet," said Shah Gebal, "I would still have had this dervish hanged if it had occurred to him to preach murder out of religious zeal in my own lands as well—"

<meta>catchword: I</meta>

<summary>In this passage, Danishmende provides a critical historical overview of the Crusades and the Knights Templar, framing the wars as the result of fanatical preaching by a "dervish" (Peter the Hermit) rather than true piety. Shah Gebal responds by condemning religious violence, suggesting that even a ruler of faith must prioritize civil peace over murderous zeal.</summary>

The more enlightened portion, therefore—those who called themselves Knights Templar—allied themselves with several so-called Essenes <note>The Essenes were an ancient Jewish sect; in the 18th century, many theorists wrongly believed they were the secret ancestors of the Freemasons.</note> during the time that their brothers were sacrificing their fellow men to a misunderstood zeal. These Essenes formed their own secret society of virtuous theists <term>theists: those who believe in a creator God through reason and nature rather than through organized religious dogma</term> and naturalists <term>naturalists: early scientists or philosophers who studied the physical laws of the world</term>. The Templars allowed themselves to be initiated by them, taking their institutions, their aims, and their discoveries back to Europe. There, they continued to maintain their bond, spreading light in secret and transforming the customs of their age. 

But soon these men rose to a level of power that became terrifying to the Sultan <note>The narrator uses 'Sultan' to refer to the French King Philip IV (the Fair) to maintain the story's Eastern atmosphere.</note> reigning at the time. Danisch. I thought it necessary, Sire, to prefix some historical reports, in order to —

In order to instruct us? said the Sultan, smiling — but do continue, dear doctor —

Danisch. It is a misfortune of humanity that even the most sacred things, in the hands of men, can never remain long in their original purity; so too here the virtuous intentions of the first founders were corrupted, and from the legislation of virtue arose despotism and superstition, and from skeptical naturalists <term>naturalists: "Naturforscher," scholars who study the physical world through observation and reason</term>, and their conjectures, [arose] the most wretched delusions <term>delusions: "Schwärmereyen," a term often used in the 18th century to describe irrational religious fervor or fanatical mysticism</term>. Men gifted with a heated imagination, fanatics and frauds banded together, and soon gained a following, because nothing is easier than selling nonsense as truth to gullible people, especially when one knows how to shroud this nonsense in enigmatic language —

I wish my Imam <note>Imam: A religious leader; Shah Gebal is suggesting a satirical comparison between the historical frauds described by Danisch and the religious authorities in his own court.</note> were here — said Shah Gebal —

Danisch. Not I—and so arose, Sire, **Theosophists**, **Alchemists**, and **Rosicrucians**—

Nurmah. Are Theosophists and Rosicrucians the same thing?—

Danisch. One and the same, and almost a single doctrine. There are various sects among them, but they agree on the main point—namely, the investigation of secret sciences. To be sure, no longer with the skeptical doubt that alone befits us mortals, but instead they cling with the most unyielding, blindest faith to miraculous delusions <term>delusions: "Schwärmereyen," a term frequently used during the Enlightenment to criticize irrational religious fervor or fanatical imagination</term> that are raised far above the sphere of human understanding, or are quite simply impossible. Among all sectarians, Sire, the Theosophist is the most difficult to dissuade from his opinions, and truly! With what weapons should a philosopher fight against these people, who refuse to argue with those natural ones—those of **reason**?—

A Theosophist, (here Danischmende turned toward the beautiful Nurmahal,

<meta>This page continues the speech of Aeschmaim (the "Doctor"). On the previous page, he began explaining that the Rosicrucians' intense "holy zeal" is the reason for their aggressive behavior toward critics. This page provides a specific example of their response to an apostate member.</meta>

...and inflames our soul for the righteous cause of our **Order**, is the reason why we can never answer our opponents except with the coarsest and most tasteless insults. For example, recently one who had left our ranks—someone who had passed through all our degrees <term>degrees: the hierarchical stages of initiation within a secret society</term>—claimed he found nothing therein but fanaticism <note>original: "Schwärmereyen." In the 18th century, this was a common derogatory term for irrational religious enthusiasm or mystical delusions.</note> and political schemes for our own expansion. Against his own better knowledge and conscience, he published a book against us, in which—I must confess—he raised some desperate doubts against us.

*Danisch.* As for their Kabbalah <term>Kabbalah; here used in a broad Enlightenment-era sense to refer to mystical, secret, or magical traditions</term> and their miracle-workings, people can easily imagine that God will not change the arrangement of His eternal laws of nature just to please a few individuals. One always pairs the concept with the thought: a miracle; a supernatural thing. But in doing so, one does not consider that it amounts to the same as: an impossible thing. For, what does one understand by the word Nature? Certainly nothing other than the eternal arrangement of things. Therefore, a miracle would be impossible within this arrangement. And for the most part, these so-called miracles are useless, and their cause is ridiculous. If it pleased the eternal Deity to convince me, He would not need to blind my eyes. — His power is capable of enlightening my spirit. — He does not need to throw the laws of nature into a heap <note>original: *über den Hauffen zu werffen*; a German idiom meaning to overturn, discard, or ruin</note>; He can tune my head and my heart in such a way that it becomes impossible for me, [cer- / a-]

--- Page 61 ---
<meta>This page continues Danischmende's critique of supernaturalism. The previous page concluded with the human desire to "consult the mirror of the future" and see the "entire chain of existence" in a single glance. This page explores the psychological consequences of that frustrated desire.</meta>

This restless desire gives birth to all **fanatical phenomena** <term>original: *schwärmerische Phänomena*; refers to the delusions or visionary experiences of those who claim direct divine inspiration or supernatural insight</term>: the ever-active **imagination** always attempts to tear the **veil of the future**, and man becomes a **fanatic** <note>original: *Schwärmer*; in the 18th century, this term described someone misled by religious enthusiasm, mystical fervor, or delusions of the supernatural</note> because he cannot become a **prophet**.

The **inclination toward the miraculous** <note>literally "the wonderful"</note> implanted within us is sufficient to preserve our **credulity** <term>original: *Glaubsucht*; literally a "longing or addiction to belief," implying a tendency to believe without evidence</term> against all the principles of **skepticism**: the imagination, when left to its own devices, loves to heap wonder upon wonder, and then defends against the **philosopher** those monsters it has brought forth, simply because they are its own creation.

**Legislators** and **sectarians** knew that one can always lead mankind by performing great and marvelous spectacles before them, surprising the imagination rather than enlightening the **reason** — It is but an imperceptible step from admiration to **fanatical faith**: the philosopher alone doubts, for he is wise enough not to trust even what he sees and hears, and much less the works of his own imagination.

<sig>It</sig>

<summary>Danischmende argues that human fanaticism arises from a frustrated desire to know the future, which leads the imagination to create "monsters" and miracles. He suggests that leaders often exploit this psychological trait by using spectacles to bypass reason, while the philosopher remains skeptical of even his own senses.</summary>

<keywords>fanaticism, imagination, prophet, skepticism, credulity, miracles, reason, philosophy, religious enthusiasm, psychology</keywords>

# Reception.

---

<margin>1.</margin> No other brothers are admitted to this high level <note>original: *Stuffe*; literally "step" or "stair," used here to denote a degree or rank within the order.</note> than Scottish <term>A. M.</term> <note>original: *Schottische A. M.*; likely an abbreviation for *Andreas Meister* (St. Andrew’s Masters), a high-ranking degree in 18th-century German Freemasonry.</note> who have given sufficient proof of their fear of God, integrity, love for humanity, and a thirst for knowledge of wisdom.

2. When such a Master <note>original: *M.*; abbreviation for *Meister*.</note> has been found, the leader shall determine for him the day and the hour of his reception; and after he has knocked at the antechamber as a <term>S. M.</term> <note>original: *S. M.*; likely *Schottischer Meister* (Scottish Master).</note>, he shall be let in, and there be received by a theoretical brother <note>A member of the "Theoretical" degree, which was the first grade of the Order of the Golden and Rosy Cross.</note> with the following address:

3. “Be greeted three times three from the heart, dear brother!” And after he has demanded the Scottish sign, the grip <note>original: *Berührung*; literally "touch," referring to the secret handshake or "grip" used for identification.</note>, and the word from the candidate, they shall give each other the customary fourfold kiss.

## The Oath.

I, <note>The placeholder "N. N." stands for the Latin *nomen nescio*, where the candidate would insert his full name.</note> vow freely and with careful consideration:

<margin>☒</margin>
1. As long as I live, to worship the eternal, almighty Jehovah <note>The Tetragrammaton or Hebrew name of God, frequently used in 18th-century esoteric circles to emphasize a return to original, biblical roots.</note> in spirit and in truth.

2. To strive as much as possible to recognize His omnipotence and wisdom through nature. <note>This reflects "Natural Theology," the belief that God can be understood by studying the physical world and its laws.</note>

3. To renounce the vanities of the world.

4. As much as it lies within my power, to look after the best interests of my brothers, to love them, to assist them with counsel and deed in all times of need, and finally a...

# Remarks.

---

1. After the initiation <note>original: "Reception"</note>, the Worshipful Senior Overseer <term>W. O. V. (Wohl-Ehrwürdiger Ober-Vorsteher): The title for the presiding officer or Master of the lodge in this specific rite.</term> shall read to the newly admitted Brother <note>original: "B." # Laws

## §. 2.
## On Religion.

Every theoretical brother <term>theoretical brother: A member of the "Theoreticus" degree, the second rank in the Order of the Golden and Rosy Cross.</term> must be devoted to a certain religion and diligently and eagerly fulfill its requirements, as is consistent with the duties of an honest man. Furthermore, every man is free to give his assent to those opinions which he considers the most rational and beneficial, yet only to such opinions as make a man pious, fair, honest, kind-hearted, and helpful toward his neighbor. Moreover, it is sufficient if he be of Christian descent and faith, from whatever land he may otherwise be. <note>While the Order allowed for different denominations, it strictly required members to be Christians, reflecting the "Christian Cabala" and Hermetic traditions of the 18th century.</note>

## §. 3.
## On the Submissiveness Owed to the Authorities.

Every member of these societies must be a loyal subject of his country

For this reason, he must take extreme care never to involve himself in any rebellions <note>original: "Empörungen"</note> harmful to the state. And for this very reason in general, as well as because of their always observed blameless conduct, this society has been supported by kings, princes, and states from time immemorial.

### §. 5.

### On the Order which the Brothers should maintain in the Assembly.

It is presupposed that the assembly <term>assembly: The original German "Versammlung" refers to the formal meeting of the lodge members.</term> consists of purely worthy and knowledge-seeking brothers; consequently, the Worshipful O. V. <note>original: "O. V." stands for *Ober-Vorsteher*, the presiding officer or Chief Overseer of the lodge.</note> expects brotherly love, peace and unity, attentiveness, and a moral conduct which can serve as an example for the other brothers to emulate.

a) All criticism, all nitpicking questions <note>original: "spizfindige Fragen" refers to sophistical or overly clever arguments intended to trip others up rather than seek truth.</note>, mockery, laughter, and useless chatter, as well as all generally uncivilized actions, are therefore forbidden, as all such things are contrary to brotherly love and disrupt good order.

b) The holding of the assembly shall be made known to all members several days beforehand by the V. Secretary <note>original: "V. Secret." likely refers to the *Verbindungs-Secretair* (Corresponding Secretary) or *Vereins-Secretair*.</note>, and every Brother <note>original: "Br." is a standard abbreviation for *Bruder* (Brother).</note> is requested to...

->§. 7.<-

Since we should not let our love toward our brothers be fruitless, every brother shall agree, in addition to his reception fees <term>reception fees: The "Receptionsgebühren" are the one-time dues paid by a candidate upon being initiated into this specific degree of the Order.</term>, to give a monthly contribution of -.-.- <note>The specific amount is left blank in the manuscript, likely to be filled in by the local treasury.</note>, so that in time the necessary aid can be provided to poor brothers in cases of illness and misfortune. Such conduct is in accordance with the fulfillment of our duty, and is a proven means of pleasing God.

### Remark.

1. The first sign <term>sign: In fraternal orders like the Rosicrucians, "signs" (Zeichen) are specific ritual gestures used to recognize fellow members and symbolize spiritual truths.</term> consists of raising the first three fingers of the right hand, as a sign that we believe in one great, almighty God, who fills all things in all width, breadth, and depth.

2. The second, that one places or lays these 3 fingers upon the heart, as a sign that we love and worship Him in our hearts <note>The parenthetical "lieben" (to love) in the original text appears to be a redundant clarification or a correction to the phrasing that follows.</note> with all our heart, with all our soul, and with all our strength. <note>This is a direct reference to the "Great Commandment" found in the Bible (Deuteronomy 6:5 and Luke 10:27), emphasizing the religious foundations of the Order's ritual.</note>

<summary>This page concludes the moral obligations of the "Theorist" degree and begins detailing the ritual signs, which emphasize belief in an omnipresent God and the duty to love Him with one's entire being.</summary>
<keywords>Theorists, ritual signs, Rosicrucianism, religious duty, Great Commandment, fraternal gestures</keywords>

--- Page 97 ---
<meta>This page continues the description of ritual signs and gestures for the "Theorist" degree of the Gold and Rosy Cross, transitioning into the regulations for the ceremonial banquet or "Table Assembly."</meta>

3. The grip <note>original: "Berührung" (touch/contact); in a fraternal context, this refers to the physical sign of recognition between members.</note> consists of one Brother embracing the other very closely with both hands around the middle of the body. This signifies that, among all creatures, man is the masterpiece of the Creator, and that self-knowledge—which is prefigured by this close embrace—should be his primary occupation.

## Customs
### of the Table Assembly.

---

1. The Master of Ceremonies takes care of the assembly and everything pertaining to it; the meal shall be provided with only 3 dishes. When it is time to go to the table, every Brother takes his place, and all wait standing for the W. O. B. <note>Likely an abbreviation for "Wohl-Ehrwürdiger Ober-Beaufseher" or "Worshipful Order Brother," a high-ranking presiding officer.</note> who appears a few minutes later, bringing the salt <note>The alchemical symbol $\ominus$ represents salt, which in this tradition symbolized the physical body and the principle of stability.</note> with him. Upon opening the door, he speaks:

> God be with us!

All brothers respond:

We place our hope in Him.

When the <term>Presiding Officer<note>original: "O. V." (Ober-Vorsteher), the high-ranking leader of the local assembly.</note></term> has taken his place, he asks the <term>Worshipful Secretary<note>original: "W. V. Secretair."</note></term>:

Q: Worshipful Brother, why are we gathered here?

A: To provide the body with food and drink, to edify and delight the soul with edifying instruction, and also to give to one another all the signs of brotherly love.

Q: Then let us fulfill these laudable intentions.

Then the brothers kiss one another <note>This "Kiss of Peace" was a common liturgical and fraternal gesture symbolizing unity and spiritual equality.</note>, and the Worshipful Presiding Officer begins the <term>grace<note>original: "Tischgebet" (table prayer).</note></term> as follows:

## Prayer

> O You merciful God! You who created no creature without providing it with nourishment, and who fed the Israelites in the wilderness with manna <note>A reference to the miraculous food provided to the Israelites during the Exodus (Exodus 16), often used in these rituals as a symbol of divine providence and spiritual sustenance.</note>, give us today our daily bread, so that our body may be maintained in strength.

> We most humbly beseech You, give us also the nourishment of souls, which is Your holy Word and Spirit, so that we may be provided for both physically and spiritually. We wish to praise You forever and give the glory to You alone, for You alone are the Eternal and alone Powerful One, Amen!

3. After the prayer, the brothers remain standing, and the <term>Brother Master of Ceremonies<note>original: "Br. Cerem. M." (Bruder Ceremonien-Meister), the officer responsible for the ritual flow and logistics of the lodge.</note></term> brings to the <term>Presiding Officer<note>original: "Oberv." (Obervorsteher).</note></term> a loaf of bread and a chalice with wine, and then returns to his place.

The <term>Worshipful Presiding Officer<note>original: "W. O. V." (Wohl-Ehrwürdiger Ober-Vorsteher).</note></term> breaks off a small piece of bread and gives the remainder to the brothers, so that each may take a small piece of it, and says: May God bless this bread for us! After he has drunk a little wine, he says: May God bless this wine for us. He then passes the chalice around and says: May it be for us a reminder of the covenant of inviolable fidelity and love toward our brothers.

4. Thereupon the brothers sit down and dine, but observe silence <note>Ritual silence during a meal was a common practice in both monastic and esoteric traditions, intended to foster internal contemplation and spiritual focus.</note>.

**Q.** How did this occur?

**A.** The other planets and stars all obtain their light from the sun, which acts as the general receiver <note>original: "Generalempfänger"; implying the sun is the central vessel that collects and then redistributes light.</note> of the created light. Conversely, the other stars constantly cast [light] from the essence of their own bodies back to the sun according to their order. Because of this, the sun is maintained in its power and radiance; it casts forth its rays, fills the air with them, and through the air, sends them to the sublunary bodies <term>sublunary: referring to the physical realm below the moon (the Earth), which in ancient cosmology was considered the region of change, decay, and the four elements.</term>.

**Q.** What is the elementary world?
**A.** The solid body which the Creator, during the separation of Chaos, divided as the coarsest part from the purest part.

**Q.** Was this world-structure <note>original: "Weltgebäude"; literally "world-building," referring to the universe as a grand architectural construction.</note> then immediately placed into its perfect state?
**A.** No! Instead, God first created the Spirit out of His powerful Word.

**Q.** Why the Spirit first?
**A.** Because the Spirit, both in the Godhead and in created beings, is the fundamental essence and primordial matter <note>original: "Urstof"; the first or most basic substance from which all things are formed.</note> of all things.

**Q.** What did God ordain with this Spirit at the foundation of this world?

**A.** The Almighty let this Spirit, which proceeds from Him, seek the center <note>original: "Mittelpunct"</note> of the world-structure <note>original: "Weltgebäude"; referring to the universe as a grand architectural construction.</note>, settle itself there, and contract; in this center, it could give birth to further beings from itself, and then, from the center of its dominion, expand into an appropriate circumspheric <note>original: "circumsphärische"; describing an expansion that radiates outward in a sphere from a central point.</note> width.

**Q.** Was this central contraction necessary?

**A.** Certainly; for we see with our own eyes that even now, no thing—neither animal, nor herb, nor metal—can come into being without contraction, and consequently, this world-structure would not have come to light <note>meaning to be brought into physical manifestation or existence.</note>.

**Q.** Were other beings then necessary for this world-structure?

**A.** Yes! For a simple, thin spirit could not have constituted the plurality and diversity of this world-structure; with-

**Q.** What other kind of being did the Spirit create through the aforementioned contraction?

**A.** A second kind of its essence, which we call the **Soul**.

**Q.** What is this soul called by the Solomonic Sage <note>Refers to King Solomon, traditionally regarded in Western esotericism as the author of wisdom books like Proverbs and Ecclesiastes, and a master of the secrets of nature.</note>?

**A.** ***Nephesh*** <note>original: נֶפֶשׁ</note>, which means a detached branch, because the soul was taken out of the universal World-Spirit.

**Q.** How did this branching off occur?

**A.** In the contracting movement, through the pressure of its essence, the Spirit detached a part of its own being from itself, and thus contracted it somewhat more densely than its essence itself was. This essence, branching off or separated from the Spirit, consequently also received a different power and motion.

**Q.** Was the soul, with its contracting power, sufficient to represent the great world-structure <note>original: "Weltbau"; the universe envisioned as a complex architectural feat.</note>?

--- Page 106 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue on the hierarchy of creation. The previous page established that while the Soul (Nephesh) was a denser contraction of the universal Spirit, it was still insufficient to create the physical world's diversity.</meta>

A. No, for although the aforementioned soul received a denser essence than the spirit itself, it was still far too spiritual, invisible, and simple; thus it was not yet sufficient for the diversity of this great world-structure <note>original: "Weltgebäude"; the universe envisioned as a complex architectural feat.</note>, and consequently, a third essence was still necessary.

Q. What kind of being was this?

A. A body; that is, the Earth, as the coarsest part separated from the purest light in order to be able to multiply itself, as the three kingdoms of nature <note>The traditional division of the natural world into the animal, vegetable, and mineral kingdoms.</note> prove daily. The soul is therefore only a modified essence of the spirit, and the body is a modified and denser essence of the soul.

--- Page 112 ---
<meta>This page continues the ritual catechism of the "Theoretical" degree of the Order of the Golden and Rosy Cross. It moves from the definition of a "theoretical philosopher" to a deeper investigation into the spiritual essence of Nature and the moral requirements of the initiate.</meta>

The **Worthy Presiding Officer:** What is Nature?

**Answer:** Nature is an invisible, volatile spirit, which nonetheless works visibly within bodies and has its seat in the divine will. <note>volatile spirit (German: *flüchtiger Geist*): In alchemical terms, "volatile" refers to a substance or spirit that easily evaporates or transitions between states. Here, it describes the *Spiritus Mundi* or "World Spirit" that Rosicrucians believed animated all physical matter.</note>

The **Presiding Officer:** By what symbol have the <term>Freemasons</term> represented Nature? <note>Freemasons: The Rosicrucian orders of the 18th century often recruited from Freemasonry and used its structure as a foundation, while claiming to possess "higher" or "more ancient" alchemical secrets.</note>

**Answer:** By the <note>hexagram symbol: ✡</note> which represents the divine breath, the universal and central <note>triangle symbol: △</note> which creates and animates everything. <note>The hexagram (Seal of Solomon) and the triangle were central to Rosicrucian geometry, representing the union of fire and water, or the spiritual and the material.</note>

**Question:** What qualities must the searchers of Nature possess?

**Answer:** The searchers of Nature must be constituted like Nature itself, namely: truthful, simple, patient, steadfast, pious and God-fearing, and kind-hearted and helpful toward their neighbor. All these qualities are instilled in the brothers of the lower degrees of <term>Masonry</term> at their initiation. <note>Masonry (German: *Mauerey*): Refers to the "Blue Lodge" or the three basic degrees of Freemasonry (Entered Apprentice, Fellow Craft, and Master Mason), which served as the moral prerequisite for this higher Rosicrucian degree.</note>

**Question:** Into how many regions is Nature divided?

<vocab>Nature, Freemasons, Masonry, Spirit, Divine Will, Qualities</vocab>

<summary>This page presents a Rosicrucian catechism defining Nature as a spiritual force driven by the divine will and symbolized by the hexagram. It also outlines the moral virtues—such as patience and piety—required of an initiate seeking to study these natural mysteries.</summary>
<keywords>Nature, volatile spirit, hexagram, triangle, divine breath, searchers of nature, Rosicrucian virtues, Masonry, ritual catechism</keywords>

--- Page 113 ---
<meta>This page continues the ritual catechism of the "Theoretical" degree of the Order of the Golden and Rosy Cross. It details the elementary qualities of nature, the three alchemical principles known as the Tria Prima, and concludes the formal opening of the ritual assembly.</meta>

**Answer:** In four regions: the warm, cold, dry, and moist, which are the elementary qualities through which all things are worked. <note>These four "qualities" are the Aristotelian foundations of physics used in alchemy. For example, Fire is warm and dry, while Water is cold and moist. Their balance was thought to determine the health and composition of all matter.</note>

**Question:** Into what does Nature transform itself?

**Answer:** It transforms itself into male and female, and is compared to <term>Mercury</term>. <note>original symbol: ☿. In alchemical thought, Mercury is often depicted as "hermaphroditic" or "androgyne," representing a bridge between the masculine (Sulfur/Sun) and feminine (Salt/Moon) principles.</note>

**Question:** How many beginnings does Nature have?

**Answer:** It has three beginnings, namely <term>Salt</term>, <term>Sulfur</term>, and <term>Mercury</term>, by virtue of which all things are brought forth, nourished, and maintained. <note>Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury (original symbols: 🜔, 🜍, ☿): These are the *Tria Prima* or "Three Primes" popularized by the physician Paracelsus. They represent the Body (Salt), the Soul (Sulfur), and the Spirit (Mercury).</note>

**Question:** How many kingdoms of nature are there?

**Answer:** Three: the mineral, vegetable, and animal.

**Question:** When should a philosopher begin his work?

**Answer:** At the breaking of day, thereby indicating his diligence and vigilance. <note>This is both literal (for chemical experiments requiring light) and symbolic, representing the "dawn" of spiritual enlightenment.</note>

The **Worthy Presiding Officer** strikes seven blows with the hammer upon the altar and speaks: Hereby be the assembly opened.

# Conclusion
## of the Assembly

The **W. O. V.** <note>Abbreviation for *Wuerdigster Ober-Vorsteher*, translated as Worthy Presiding Officer.</note> speaks: Worthy Brothers! Does anyone have anything to bring forward for the benefit of the assembly?

->If no one speaks up, the **W. O. V.** asks the **Brother Secretary**.<-

**Question:** When does a philosopher rest? <note>In the context of this Order, a "philosopher" refers to an initiate practicing alchemy and searching for divine wisdom.</note>

**Answer:** When the work has reached perfection. <note>The "work" refers to the *Magnum Opus* or Great Work—the process of purifying the soul and creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note>

**Question:** What time is it at the end of the work?

**Answer:** High noon; that is the moment when the sun is at its greatest strength and the moon is in its full splendor. <note>This is a symbolic time rather than a literal one. It represents the "Conjunction," or the perfect union of the solar (masculine/Sulfur) and lunar (feminine/Mercury) principles in alchemy.</note>

**Question:** What is the age of a seeking philosopher?

**Answer:** From the moment of his investigations until that of his discovery, he does not grow old. <note>This suggests that the pursuit of truth keeps the spirit in a state of perpetual youth, or perhaps refers to the life-extending properties of the Elixir of Life that "philosophers" sought.</note>

<meta>The catchword "Fr." at the bottom of the page stands for *Frage* (Question), indicating that the ritual dialogue continues on the following page.</meta>

<summary>This page details the formal closing ritual of the assembly, using a symbolic question-and-answer format to describe the alchemist's goal of perfection. It defines the successful completion of the "Great Work" as a state of eternal youth and spiritual "high noon."</summary>

<keywords>ritual closing, philosopher, Great Work, high noon, sun and moon, spiritual perfection, Golden and Rosy Cross</keywords>

--- Page 115 ---
<meta>This page continues the closing ritual of the "Theoretical" degree of the Order of the Golden and Rosy Cross. It concludes the formal question-and-answer dialogue between the Presiding Officer and the initiates before moving into the final prayer.</meta>

**Question:** Tell me the word of Magnesia? <note>In alchemy, "Magnesia" does not refer to the chemical element magnesium, but rather to the "Magnesia of the Wise"—a mysterious "First Matter" believed to be the essential starting point for the Philosopher's Stone.</note>
**Answer:** It is contained within these words: *Visit the interior of the earth; by rectifying, you will find the hidden stone, the true medicine (that is, Vitriol)*. <note>original: "Visita interiora terrae ratificando [rectificando], invenies occultum lapidem, veram medicinam (id est Vitriolum)." This is a famous alchemical motto. The first letters of each Latin word spell "VITRIOL," which symbolized the process of looking inward into one's own soul to find divine truth.</note> Will you transmit to me the sign, the grip, and the word of the philosophers?

One **Brother** gives it to the next, and the final two pass it to the **W. O. V.** <note>Abbreviation for *Wuerdigster Ober-Vorsteher*, the Worthy Presiding Officer.</note> on both sides.

The **W. O. V.** speaks: The assembly is hereby closed—and strikes the altar 7 times. <note>The number seven is highly symbolic in this tradition, representing the seven classical planets, the seven metals of alchemy, and the seven days of creation.</note> Following this, the prayer is spoken after the assembly.

# Prayer
## after the Assembly.

O God! Who is like unto You! Heaven is Your throne, and the earth is Your footstool <note>A reference to Isaiah 66:1 or Matthew 5:34-35, emphasizing God’s sovereignty over the entire universe.</note>. You, the Almighty, dwell on high in a light where no mortal can reach <note>A reference to 1 Timothy 6:16. In the Rosicrucian tradition, this "unapproachable light" represents the source of all spiritual illumination.</note>; and all who dwell on earth are before You like grasshoppers <note>A biblical metaphor from Isaiah 40:22, illustrating the vast difference between divine majesty and human insignificance.</note>. O immortal God! Through You nature lives, and Your breath gives her a soul. We humbly beseech You: teach us to rightly recognize her <note>The original German "dieselbe" refers back to Nature. The goal of the Rosicrucian "Theoreticus" was to study God through the "Book of Nature."</note>, and through Your omnipotence, to admire You in Your creatures; for You alone are powerful, You alone are great, You alone are merciful; to You alone belong praise, honor, and glory for all eternity. Amen.

---

### Instruction of the Theoretical Brothers.

The W. O. V. <note>original: "W. O. V."; likely the *Wohl Ehrwürdiger Ober-Vorsteher* or Very Honorable Superior, the presiding officer of the assembly.</note> speaks:

Since we are gathered here to instruct ourselves in Nature, and through her to better know God our Creator and ourselves, I ask: what is God?

->A Brother speaks:<-

Reverent silence befits our weak reason better than wanting to fathom God in His essence; for as finite creatures, we can neither speak nor think of this infinite Good, since a part cannot comprehend the whole. But the <term>Solomonic philosophy<gloss>The esoteric and alchemical wisdom traditionally attributed to King Solomon, central to Rosicrucian thought.</gloss></term> teaches us that God is an eternal, immeasurable, invisible Spirit, who reigns in an uncreated light, from whence

whence He has revealed His incomprehensible wisdom and omnipotence to us through the creation of the world. Through His holy Word, the invisible has become visible, and His divine breath animates all of Nature.

---

**Q.** What is Nature?

**A.** Nature is an invisible spirit, which nevertheless works visibly within bodies, and has its seat in the divine will. <note>In Rosicrucian thought, Nature is not just the physical world, but a spiritual force acting as God's agent.</note>

**Q.** From what did Nature arise?

**A.** If we consider the created works of God in general, we find that <term>Chaos<gloss>In this context, Chaos refers to the "Materia Prima" or the primordial, unorganized substance that contains the potential for all matter.</gloss></term> was and still is the fundamental beginning of all created things.

**Q.** What is Chaos?

->§. 1.<-

**A.** Chaos was a mingled mass, which was moved by water, and this [water] was enlivened by fire; from this mass, all things of this world were created and brought forth through the almighty and eternal Word of God. This

<gloss>Wisdom, Omnipotence, Nature, Spirit, Chaos, Fundamental Beginning, Creation</gloss>

<summary>This page defines Nature as an invisible spirit seated in the divine will and introduces "Chaos" as the primordial mass of water and fire from which God created the world.</summary>
<keywords>Nature, Chaos, Creation, Divine Will, Primordial Mass, Four Elements, Rosicrucian Catechism</keywords>

--- Page 120 ---
<meta>This page continues the definition of Chaos from page 99. The previous page ended with the word "This," which leads into the description of the primordial matter.</meta>

was the Matter in which all forms and shapes were contained in power (*potentially* <note>original Latin: *potentialiter*</note>), and subsequently revealed and set into action through the will of the eternal God. This formless lump, namely the Chaos, was watery, and is signified by the little Hebrew word שמים (*Shamayim* <note>original: "Schamaym." While usually translated as "Heavens," alchemists often interpreted this word as a combination of *Esh* (fire) and *Mayim* (water)</note>), which means <image-desc>interlaced triangles forming a hexagram</image-desc> and Matter; this material <term>Fire-Water<gloss>The source uses the symbol △ (Fire), though it describes it as water. In alchemy, this often refers to "Philosophical Water" or a "Fiery Water" that acts as the universal solvent.</gloss></term> was divided by God into the upper, middle, and lower. The upper part is perfectly enlightened and very subtle <note>In this context, "subtle" refers to matter that is refined, volatile, or spiritualized rather than dense.</note> compared to all others; the lowest part, on the other hand, is perfectly dark, impure, coarse, and thick. The middle part has something of both and is mixed with the upper and the lower. The lower part, or the third class, nevertheless comprises and contains within itself the properties, virtues, powers, and essences of all beings of the creatures of the first class, yet with this distinction: that what the lower creatures are actually in manifest form, the upper creatures are in a potential power and hidden essence.

On the other hand, the upper part is so constituted that nothing is to be found in the lower part whose nature, virtue, and powers the upper part does not encompass and contain within itself. What the upper beings are outwardly and manifest, the lower are inwardly and hidden; yet the creatures, or created beings, of the upper and lower parts are widely different in their working.

For the upper, spiritual, and intelligent creatures can, if they wish, work just as the lower creatures do; but the lower creatures, because of their coarse and dark bodies, are prevented from being able to perform any such spiritual work by themselves, like the angels, unless they are enlightened from above and endowed more with divine than human <term>virtuous powers<gloss>German: "Tugend-Kräfte." ### Concerning the Elements in General.

The element is a separate body [formed] from the <term>Chaos<gloss>In alchemy, "Chaos" refers to the "Prima Materia" or the primordial, unformed state of matter before it is organized into specific elements.</gloss></term>, in and through which elemented things <note>Things composed of or governed by the elements.</note> exist; or it is the beginning of a thing, just as a letter is the beginning of a syllable.

The science and teaching of the elements is highly important and necessary; for it is the key to the sacred mysteries of nature, without which everything remains locked away. The elements have a precise kinship and communion with one another and easily transform from one into another, as we intend to show hereafter.

Earth is transformed into <term>Water<gloss>original symbol: $\nabla$. The alchemical symbol for the element of water.</gloss></term>, as the <term>Water<gloss>original symbol: $\nabla$</gloss></term>—driven by moving warmth—presses out from the center of the earth, permeates the earth, [and turns] it into the form of a <meta>catchword: Vap-</meta> <note>The catchword "Dam-" at the bottom of the German page refers to "Dampf," meaning "vapor" or "steam."</note>

...be separated from all inequality. <note>The author is describing a state of elemental perfection where the four elements—Fire, Air, Water, and Earth—no longer conflict but exist in a state of "Gleichheit" or perfect balance.</note> The movement, which time and its differences currently cause, will then be equally incessant and nothing other than eternity itself. ### §. 3.
## On the Special Elements, and specifically regarding Fire <note>original: $\triangle$, the alchemical symbol for Fire.</note>.

1. **Fire** <note>original: $\triangle$</note> and **Air** <note>original: 🜁, the alchemical symbol for Air.</note> are the upper elements. Fire, being the first, precedes all others because of its purity, <term>subtlety</term> <note>In alchemy, "subtlety" (Dünnigkeit) refers to the quality of being refined, spirit-like, and able to penetrate other substances.</note>, and perfection, which it attains from its <term>simplicity</term> <note>A philosophical term meaning the state of being unmixed or non-composite; the more "simple" a substance is, the closer it is to the divine.</note>; for this reason, it has become nobler and more powerful than the others. The universal **World-Spirit** <note>original: *Weltgeist*, often referred to as the *Spiritus Mundi*, a hypothetical substance believed to permeate the universe and facilitate the influence of the stars on earthly matter.</note> has its seat in this element and imparts wonderful powers to the element of Fire.

2. **Air**, because it is not as pure as **Fire**, can never thoroughly penetrate the element of Fire, nor unite itself entirely with Fire, unless it has first been cleansed of its impurity.

3. Elementary **Fire** acts only when it is concentrated and driven together; this happens when its rays are strengthened and their <term>emanations</term> <note>original: *Ausflüsse*; the outward flowing of energy or spiritual substance from a source.</note> are cast powerfully from it.

4. When the almighty **Creator** [created] the elements (Genesis 1:10) and all <term>elemented</term> <note>original: *elementirte*; referring to physical things composed of the four elements.</note> 

<meta>catchword: things</meta>

<summary>This page introduces the elemental theory of the cosmos, identifying Fire and Air as the "upper elements" and detailing the spiritual and physical supremacy of Fire as the seat of the World-Spirit.</summary>
<keywords>Fire, Air, World-Spirit, simplicity, subtlety, creation, Genesis, elemental theory</keywords>

### §. 4.
### On the Air.

1. The **Air** <term>🜁</term> is a very subtle, transparent, invisible, and light element; it is the bond between the upper and lower things; a dwelling place for aerial signs and meteors <note>In the 17th and 18th centuries, "meteors" referred to any atmospheric phenomena, such as rain, rainbows, or lightning, not just falling stars.</note>. No thing in the world can do without this element, for from it all creatures draw nourishment and life; it strengthens their generative sap and nourishes the vital spirits. Indeed, nothing in this world would be born if the **Air** <term>🜁</term> did not penetrate all things and attract the multiplying power of nourishment to itself. The **Air** <term>🜁</term>, says Sendivogius <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636) was a famous Polish alchemist and physician who proposed that air contains a "hidden food of life" (nitre).</note>, has within it a hidden food of life, whose coagulated **salt** <term>🜔</term> <note>original: *coagulirter 🜔*; the symbol 🜔 represents Salt (Sal). Alchemists believed a spiritual salt could be extracted from the air to create the Philosopher's Stone.</note> is better than the entire inhabited world.

2. This element is purer than **Water** <term>🜄</term>, but not as pure as the heavens or **Fire** <term>🜂</term>; it partakes in the purity of the upper element and is richly endowed with the universal spirit of the world <term>spirit of the world: *Weltgeist*, the vital animating force that permeates all nature.</term>.

**Vocabulary:** Air, Element, Aerial signs, Meteors, Nourishment, Generative sap, Vital spirits, Sendivogius, Food of life, Spirit of the world, 🜁 (Air), 🜄 (Water), 🜂 (Fire), 🜔 (Salt)

<summary>This page defines the element of Air as a vital, invisible substance that connects the spiritual and physical realms and provides the essential "food of life" for all living creatures. It emphasizes Air's role as a vessel for the "universal spirit" and cites the alchemist Sendivogius regarding the hidden powers found within it.</summary>
<keywords>element of air, Michael Sendivogius, vital spirits, food of life, alchemical symbols, world spirit, atmospheric phenomena, generative sap</keywords>

### §. 5

# On Water.

1. The **Water** <term>▽</term> and the **Earth** <term>🜃</term> are the lower elements. The elevation <note>In an alchemical context, "elevation" refers to the process of purifying a substance or raising its "vibrational" state through circulation or distillation.</note> of both depends on the excellence of the upper elements <note>The "upper elements" are Air and Fire.</note>, and it is especially required for the attainment of their perfection that they be frequently elevated and enriched by the upper powers. It is required, I say, that the **Earth** <term>🜃</term> be frequently elevated by means of the **Water** <term>▽</term>, so that the **Fire** <term>△</term>, which is located in the innermost part of the earth, may appear and be made actual <note>This refers to the theory of a central, hidden fire within the earth (the *central sun*) that drives all growth and geological change.</note>. The **Water** <term>▽</term> never returns empty to the earth without bringing some new powers with it and imparting them to the **Earth** <term>🜃</term>.

2. Rain has a greater effect upon the seed-fields than the common **water** <term>▽</term> with which gardeners irrigate. The **Water** <term>▽</term> would not penetrate the **Earth** <term>🜃</term> if it were not impregnated with upper and lower warmth; just as happens in summer, when the warmth of the internal and external sun <note>The "external sun" is the celestial sun, while the "internal sun" is the heat found within the Earth's core.</note> subtilizes <note>subtilizes: to refine a substance, making it more fluid, volatile, or spiritually active so it can penetrate solid matter.</note> the **Water** <term>▽</term>, so that it [enters] into the...

<vocab>Elemente (elements), Wasser (water), Erde (earth), Feuer (fire), Regen (rain), Saamenfelder (seed-fields), Wärme (warmth/heat), Sonne (sun), subtilisiret (subtilizes), Fürtreflichkeit (excellence)</vocab>

<summary>This page explains the alchemical relationship between Water and Earth, describing how Water acts as a vessel that carries celestial "powers" down to the Earth to activate its internal fire. It highlights the superior life-giving quality of rainwater, which is refined by the combined heat of the celestial and terrestrial suns.</summary>

...penetrates the roots of plants, digesting <note>In alchemy, "digesting" (original: *digeriren*) refers to the use of gentle, consistent heat to ripen, decompose, or transform a substance, mimicking the way the stomach processes food or the sun matures a plant.</note> them in order to enable them to grow into plants, flowers, and fruits.

3. Heat draws moisture upward like a mist, which, once it has been elevated, falls back down due to its weight in the form of raindrops and gives the earth the moisture by which it is made fertile; for this universal seawater is impregnated by the heavens <note>The author implies that as water evaporates and falls as rain, it absorbs "astral" or heavenly influences, making it more potent than ordinary ground water.</note> and therefore brings new powers down with it.

This element also contains the other three <note>The author refers to the Hermetic belief that each element contains the "seeds" or qualities of the other three (Fire, Air, and Earth) within its own nature.</note> within itself, and serves to produce, maintain, and multiply all bodies that we see.

Alchemists believed that nothing could be reborn or transformed without first breaking down and "dying" through this moist heat.</note>, which is a mother of birth; for without Water <term>▽</term>, no putrefaction can occur.

1. This is the lowest element—thick, impure, dry—the dwelling place of animals, plants, metals, and minerals. 2. The most important thing to be considered here are the <term>three primary beginnings<gloss>The "Tria Prima" of alchemy: Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury.</gloss></term> of all things. These primary beginnings are actual substances which are extracted from the element according to the temperament <note>In this context, "temperament" refers to the specific "tempering" or balance of qualities (hot, cold, moist, dry) unique to a specific substance.</note> of each thing, and have been developed into an elemented body. These three primary beginnings we call **Salt** ($\ominus$), **Sulfur** ($\unicode{x1F70D}$), and **Mercury** ($\unicode{x263F}$).

3. In bodies where these principles are well-tempered and proportioned, they create a durable substance; however, in bodies where the opposite is found, the substance is impure and fragile. Purity consists in the harmony and proportion of these three beginnings, while impurity consists in their inequality.

## On Salt.

1. **Salt** ($\ominus$) is a **fixed substance** and a fundamental beginning of all things. It is comparable to <term>airy earth<gloss>An alchemical term for a substance that possesses the solidity of earth but the subtle, penetrative qualities of air.</gloss></term>; it nourishes **Sulfur** ($\unicode{x1F70D}$) and **Mercury** ($\unicode{x263F}$), which produce their effects within it. This process continues until the Sulfur and Mercury have made the Salt as <term>volatile<gloss>In alchemy, a "volatile" substance is one that easily evaporates or changes state, as opposed to a "fixed" substance which remains stable under heat.</gloss></term> as they are and have brought it to their same level of perfection. The Salt, on the other hand, holds and **coagulates** <note>original: "coagulirt"; the process of solidifying a liquid or gas.</note> the two of them, imparting to them a fixed and dry nature; it binds together everything that is liquid. When the Salt has been dissolved into a suitable **liquid** <note>original: "Liquorem"; here referring to a specialized alchemical solvent.</note>, it then also helps to dissolve all dense bodies, just as on the other hand its fixed nature **consolidates** <note>original: "consolidirt"; to make firm or stable.</note> all open bodies. When it has been dissolved along with Sulfur and Mercury, its increasing power then gains strength, and its potential is put into action.

2. Salt and Sulfur preserve bodies from **putrefaction** <note>original: "Faulung"; the biological rotting process.</note> and dry out all superfluous moisture that can cause **decomposition**. This Salt which the

## §. 10.
# On Sulfur.

**Sulfur** is an oily and fatty fundamental beginning, which binds together the two other distinct fundamental beginnings—namely, the Dry and the Moist—and serves as a **central bond** <note>original: "Mittelbandes"; an intermediary or bridge between two opposing forces.</note>. **Sulfur** ($\unicode{x1F70D}$) possesses something of both other substances, in that it <term>partakes<gloss>original: "participiret"; to share in the nature or qualities of something else.</gloss></term> in the solidity of **Salt** ($\ominus$) and the moisture of **Mercury** ($\unicode{x263F}$). **Sulfur** ($\unicode{x1F70D}$) dries out and consumes everything superfluous, and therefore it **coagulates** <note>original: "coaguliret"; to thicken or solidify a liquid into a mass.</note> the **Mercury** ($\unicode{x263F}$); however, it cannot accomplish this coagulation alone without **Salt** ($\ominus$);

### §. 11.
## On Mercury.

This is a **spiritual** <note>original: "spiritualischer"; in this context, it refers to a refined, vaporous, or airy essence rather than something strictly religious.</note>, thin, and subtle **liquor** <term>liquor<gloss>original: "Liquor"; a fluid substance or distilled essence.</gloss></term>, which always carries a little of the **Sulfur** ($\unicode{x1F70D}$) with and within itself. ### §. 14.

## On Preservation.

The **preservation** <term>Erhaltung; the process by which a created thing maintains its life and form over time.</term> of natural things occurs through the very same power as their **generation** <term>Gebährung; the initial birth or creation of a substance or being.</term>, yet with this difference: preservation is accomplished through the acceptance and enjoyment of external matter. In the consumed nourishment, two types of matter are found: one which is accepted by nature as suitable and is united and incorporated with it, while the other, being contrary to nature, is cast away.

2. The **nutritive power** <note>original: "Nahrungskraft"; the innate ability of a living or natural body to extract what it needs to survive.</note>, which effects this preservation, is both spiritual and material. The first is invisible and incomprehensible within the food; the other, however, is visible, comprehensible, and also not as fine or penetrating as the former. This pure essence, however, is nothing other than the **universal World-Spirit** <note>original: "allgemeine Weltgeist"; often referred to in alchemy as the *Spiritus Mundi*, a subtle substance that links the divine and the physical, acting as a vitalizing force.</note> dwelling within it, which, like a structure, [unites] the invisible with

### On Destruction.

1. The destruction of **elemental things** <term>elementirten Dinge; physical objects composed of the four classical elements: earth, air, fire, and water.</term> commonly occurs through the influence of a thing’s opposite, specifically when one quality dominates and overcomes another. This is accomplished either through **dissolution** <term>Dissolution; the process of a solid being dissolved into a liquid state.</term> or **coagulation** <term>Coagulation; the process by which a liquid or "spirit" becomes solid or fixed.</term>. The coarser **dissolving** <term>Dissolvirung</term> happens through physical means such as grinding, breaking, and the like; the subtler kind, however, occurs through a gentle and natural **dissolution** <term>Auflösung</term> of bodies. Through this process, such bodies are transformed into a constant and perfect nature. In this state, the **coagulation** <term>Coagulirung</term> of the new body causes the destruction of the old one. Let this be enough said regarding the three **accidents of nature** <term>Zufällen der Natur; in historical philosophy, "accidents" are the non-essential or changeable qualities of a substance, such as its shape or state, rather than its core essence.</term>; we must now consider further

## §. 16.

### The Effect of the Celestial Stars.

1. From the **celestial stars** <term>obern Gestirnen; the heavenly bodies, including planets and stars, believed in alchemy to emit a physical and spiritual "influence" that shapes life on Earth.</term> come the influences for the propagation of the various **casings** <term>Gehäuße; the physical bodies or "shells" of organisms that contain their inner spiritual essence.</term> in the **three kingdoms** <term>drey Reichen; the three traditional divisions of nature: animal, vegetable, and mineral.</term>, according to the properties of each. The light of every body among the celestial stars does not rest; instead, it is always working and striving to draw the lower light—which is found in various **seed-bodies** <term>Saamenkörpern; the material structures, such as seeds or ores, that contain the potential for new life or metallic growth.</term>—upward and toward itself. In the same manner, the lower **essence of light** <term>Lichtessenz</term> within the seeds works to pull the upper light down toward itself. Through this mutual upward and downward attraction of the upper and lower light, the movement and **growth** <term>Wachsthum</term> of all natural things occurs. This **influence** <term>Influenz; from the Latin 'influere', meaning to flow in; here referring to a subtle fluid or vapor emitted by stars.</term> of the stars is a subtle body which is capable of communicating itself to lower things by means of its rays. When these influences are simple—that is, coming from a single star—their effect is also simple; however, an influence mingled with many rays acts differently upon the lower bodies, in that it either accelerates their action,

Through this mutual drawing up and pulling down of the upper and lower light, the movement and growth of all natural things occurs. This influence of the stars is a **subtle body** <term>subtiler Körper; a substance that is neither purely spiritual nor purely solid, acting as a medium through which heavenly forces can affect the physical earth.</term> which is able to communicate itself to lower things by means of rays. When these influences are simple—that is, from a single star—then their effect is also merely simple; but **influence** <term>Influenz; derived from the Latin "influentia," meaning a "flowing in." In this context, it refers to the liquid-like transmission of celestial power into the terrestrial realm.</term> mixed with many rays operates diversely in the lower bodies, in that it either accelerates the operation of the same...

### Section 19.
## Of the Birth of Metals.

### §. 29.
# On the Diseases of Humanity.
## Firstly, on the Diseases of the Body.

1. A **disease** is essentially nothing other than a destruction and corruption of the **natural balsam** <term>Balsam: In the medical traditions of this period (likely influenced by Paracelsus), "balsam" refers to an internal, life-preserving power or fluid that keeps the body healthy and allows it to heal itself.</term> in a part of the human body. When one disease strikes another, such is called a **symptom** <note>original: "Zufall"; in early modern medicine, this refers to a secondary complication or an "accident" of nature that occurs alongside a primary illness.</note>.

2. The **external ailments** and **accidents** of man occur through a dissolution of the composite parts; namely, when an external part of the body is injured by a wound, ulcer, dislocation, fracture, or bite. The woun- <note>The text ends mid-word (Wunde/Wunden), leading into the next page.</note>

These recounted causes awaken an imbalance in the natural balsam of life <note>original: "Lebensbalsam." In early modern medicine, this was the vital fluid or "mummy" believed to sustain health and prevent the body from rotting.</note> and corrupt it; as will be further seen in the following sections.

1. Indigestion in the human body occurs when the chyle <note>original: "Speis- und Nahrungssaft," the milky fluid formed in the stomach and intestines during the first stage of digestion.</note> or the blood has not been properly "cooked" and refined. This is brought about by the excessive, unhealthy, and varied consumption of food and drink, for the ferment in the stomach and its digestive power <note>original: "Kochungskraft," literally "cooking power." Medieval and early modern physicians viewed digestion as a process of "coction" or cooking driven by internal body heat.</note> are thereby weakened. The neglect of physical exercise also contributes significantly to this, especially when the natural heat is not stimulated and exercised. Such indigestion then causes many grievances within the body. In the stomach, it creates a loathing of food or a disordered craving to eat earth, chalk, charcoal, and the like <note>This condition is known as *pica*, a disorder where patients crave non-food substances, often attributed in historical texts to an imbalance of the stomach's humors.</note>. All such diseases are prevented and cured: first, through external **exercise of the limbs** <note>original: "Gliederübung," referring to physical activity or movement therapy.</note>; secondly, through a good and fragrant **fumigation** <note>original: "Beräucherung," the medical practice of inhaling or exposing the body to medicinal smoke or vapors.</note> of the brain; thirdly, with a warm and sulfurous air; and then finally, with a good regimen in food and drink. ### 4. Concerning Obstruction.
Such **obstruction** <note>original: "Verstopfung," referring here to the blockage of any of the body's internal channels or vessels, not just the bowels.</note> likewise causes many kinds of ailments. In the intestines, it creates **intestinal colic** <note>original: "Darmgicht," literally "gut-gout," a term used for severe abdominal pain or blockages.</note>; in the liver, it causes **dropsy** <note>original: "Wassersucht," the historical term for swelling caused by fluid accumulation (edema).</note>, because the <term>nutritive juice <gloss>original: "Speisesaft," referring to chyle, the milky fluid produced during digestion.</gloss></term> cannot be properly "cooked" <note>Early modern medicine often described digestion as a process of "concoction" or cooking by the body's internal heat.</note> into blood, and therefore it does not **nourish** <note>original: "nutrificiret," derived from the Latin *nutrificare*.</note>. In the gallbladder, it brings about **jaundice**; in the spleen, it causes **black jaundice** <note>original: "schwarze Gelbsucht," a historical diagnosis likely related to an excess of black bile or severe metabolic dysfunction.</note>, because such obstructed organs cannot function, and then their juices pour into the bloodstream, which is colored by them. In the kidneys and in the bladder, obstruction causes **gravel and stone**, which block the other [vessels] and **urinary passages**.

### 5.

### §. 32.
## On the Correspondence of All Things.

1. Everything that has been said in this entire work is nothing other than a correspondence <note>original: "Uebereinstimmung," which can also mean harmony or agreement; it refers here to the Hermetic principle of "as above, so below"</note> of the internal with the external, the above with the below, and the spiritual with the physical. I also do not doubt, my brothers <note>the author likely addresses a specific fraternal or spiritual community, such as the Rosicrucians</note>, that they will understand this sufficiently without further report. Nevertheless, in order to provide them with guidance therein, I will report only a few things here in conclusion.

2. All creatures are entirely different in terms of their form, but in terms of their matter, they are not much different from one another; therefore, they have a great kinship with each other.

3. The elements, because they all originate from a single Chaos <note>here referring to the *materia prima* or the primordial, unformed state of matter</note>, have no other distinction among themselves than according to the manner of their disposition and exaltation <term>Erhöhung: in an alchemical or philosophical context, this refers to the refining, purifying, or raising of a substance to a higher, more potent state</term>. All things are enclosed in Unity and return back to it. This consideration...

<meta>Continuing from page 212, the text completes the word "Betrachtung" (consideration/observation) and continues the discussion on the correspondence of all things, specifically focusing on the shared mechanics of growth and preservation across the natural kingdoms.</meta>

...consideration <note>Completing the word "Betrachtung" from the previous page</note> gives the right key to the very greatest secrets of Nature, in which we see how everything has been created and placed in time, measure, and weight <note>A reference to the biblical Wisdom of Solomon 11:20, "Thou hast ordered all things in measure and number and weight," a favorite quote for early modern scientists and alchemists</note>.

If you further observe the birth, nourishment, and destruction or dissolution of the three kingdoms <note>The three kingdoms of nature: animal, vegetable, and mineral</note>, you will see that in this matter they are all in agreement and coincide with one another. They take their growth from the three fundamental principles of Nature, where the active part is the male, and the passive part is the female. This growth occurs through the internal heat of the seed and by the aid of external cooking-heat <term>Kochungswärme: from the Latin *coctio*, this refers to a slow, transformative heating process that matures or ripens a substance</term>. It does not matter that their origins are distinct, just as creatures are also manifold among themselves. ### Concerning the First Labors and the Better Preparation of Mercury <term>☿</term>. <note>Mercury, or Quicksilver, represented by the symbol ☿, was considered the fundamental "mother" of all metals in alchemical theory</note>

Regarding this, it is to be known beforehand: that according to the natural knowledge of our ancient wise masters, Mercury <term>☿</term> is a viscous water of a very subtle essence, which in the veins of the earth, together with a white, very fine earth, by means of an extremely moderate heat, has been mingled and mixed most inwardly in its smallest parts, and indeed for so long; until the moisture of the water and the dryness of the earth [reached] a perfectly equal proportion and a completely inseparable union

<summary>This page concludes a spiritual exhortation and begins the practical alchemical instruction, defining Mercury as a balanced union of moisture and earth formed deep within the ground.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, Mercury, 3rd Degree, Triune One, mysteries, viscous water, mineral formation, Rosicrucian practice</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of Mercury (☿) from page 222, describing its physical and metaphysical properties after it has reached a state of "inseparable union."</meta>

prospered. Therefore, its parts do not tend to separate easily through the force of fire, as is observed in other **mixtures** <note>original: "*Mixtis*"; refers to compounded substances where components can be easily distilled or burned away separately</note>, but rather it either remains firm with all its parts and entirely within the fire, or it rises up with all its parts and vanishes; which latter effect the books of the profane **sophists** <note>A term often used by alchemists to describe those who approach chemistry without spiritual understanding or those who follow "false" paths</note> confirm through only too common testimony. Likewise, it runs upon a **horizontal surface** at the slightest movement, owing to the fluidity of its water, without wetting or—by virtue of its viscous property—clinging to it; because it is prevented from doing so by the perfectly equal counter-proportion of the dryness of its earth; for which reason our people have called it a **dry water**. This dry water, however, was destined in the arrangement of its generation to the essence of a **solid gold**, to which perfection its nature would have brought it over time; if it [had] not, by chance, [been deprived] of its **active principle** <note>original: "*Principii*"</note>, that is, its **spiritual sulfur**, or active fire...

This **spiritual sulfur** <term>the active, masculine principle in alchemy, representing the "soul" or internal fire of a substance</term>, however, as the **active principle** <note>original: "wirkende Principium"; the driving force behind chemical or spiritual transformation</note>, our sharp-witted old masters—after long contemplation of all nature in all its kingdoms, and after countless natural experiments which they undertook in all the relevant **subjects** <note>original: "Subjectis"; the specific materials or substances being experimented upon</note>—have unanimously judged:

that it is to be found in the **mineral kingdom** <note>In alchemy, nature was divided into the animal, vegetable, and mineral kingdoms; the masters believed the "Stone" must share the nature of the metals it transforms</note> alone, and consequently must be sought there and nowhere else. Therefore, they initially sought it in **Gold** <term>☉</term>, but found it to be an exceedingly tightly locked body; wherein nature has indeed produced it to all the perfection of which she is capable in her ordinary course, but by no means in a greater proportion than its own **stability** <note>original: "Selbstbeständigkeit"; referring to the "fixity" of gold, which is so perfect and stable that it does not easily release its internal essence</note> requires.

And thus they were convinced by experience that **Gold** <term>☉</term> has nothing but what it needs for itself, and consequently can give nothing up for their purpose. ...essence, by virtue of which it even purifies **gold**—when passed through it several times—to the highest degree, and also **graduates** <note>In alchemy, to "graduate" a metal means to improve its quality, specifically to intensify its color and density, making it appear more "mature" or "pure."</note> its color significantly. They abandon their own mineral water and their fine earth of golden quality, which sink to the bottom. At the same time, they attract and swallow the **mercurial water** of **Mars** <term>♂, iron</term>, its **metallic salt**, and its **solar**, spiritual, fiery **sulfur** <note>original: "♂tis ☿lisches Wasser... solarisch geistig feurigen Schwefel"; the text is describing the extraction of the "soul" or essence of iron using antimony</note> as things of their own kind. 

This occurs because, in this operation, the sulfurous-combustible and arsenical parts of **Antimony** <term>♁</term> are saturated by seizing the coarse parts of the **raw iron**; however, these parts are much too coarse to be capable of retaining the aforementioned fine essence of **Mars** <term>♂</term>.

This process is likened to the way gold is refined and its color intensified through similar alchemical means.</summary>

## Of the 3rd Labor, and the Better Preparation 
## of the *Aviculae* <note>original: "Avicularum"; Latin for "little birds." In alchemy, this usually refers to volatile substances that sublimate or "fly" to the top of a vessel, or specific crystalline structures.</note>.

The *Aviculae* <note>little birds</note> are indeed prepared rightly and well according to the instruction received in the Constitution <note>likely referring to a foundational rulebook or specific alchemical treatise used by the author's order</note>, and the prepared Mercury <note>original: "☿"</note> can be quite suitably animated <note>the process of "awakening" mercury to make it reactive for the Great Work</note> with them; however, they presuppose a well-practiced fire-worker <note>original: "Feuerarbeiter"; a laboratory adept skilled in managing furnace temperatures</note>, because they are exceedingly prone to burning and hardening. This difficulty, however, can be bypassed in the following way:

> "Melt 4 ounces <note>original: "℥iiij"</note> of our Regulus <note>original: "🜿"; the metallic button found at the bottom of a crucible after smelting</note> with 2 ounces of fine cupelled Silver <note>original: "Capell ☽ r"; silver purified through cupellation</note>, and while in good flux <note>a molten, flowing state</note>, add a bit of the aforementioned flux made from common fused Salt <note>original: "🜔 com. fus"</note> and red Antimony <note>original: "♁ r"</note>. Let it stand for 1/4 hour <note>original: "⌛"</note> in the fire <note>original: "△en"</note> in the usual manner <note>original: "m. n. a."; likely an abbreviation for *more nostro assueto*, meaning "in our accustomed way"</note>; then pour it out, wash the Regulus of Silver <note>original: "🜿 ☽nae"</note> until it is perfectly clean, dry it, and process it into a fine powder with Antimony <note>original: "♁ret"</note>."

With this method, one does not need to make *Aviculae*, but instead proceeds immediately to the manipulation.

At the very instant it begins to hiss and rustle, the mixture will penetrate it like a flash of lightning. # Experimental Instructions
## or
## Necessary Preparatory Processes for the Philosophical Work, [detailing] how the radical and universal solvents <term>menstrua</term> <note>original: Menstrua; powerful alchemical liquids used to dissolve solids into their primary, "radical" state</note> and dissolving agents <term>resolventia</term> <note>original: Reſolventia</note> must be prepared from the mineral, vegetable, and animal kingdoms.

---

### First Process.
### On the preparation of the mineral radical solvent.

Take: One pound of <term>water</term> <note>original symbol: ▽ (Aqua)</note> made from equal parts <term>copper-vitriol</term> <note>original symbol: ♀ (Venus); likely referring here to vitriol of copper</note> and <term>saltpeter</term> <note>original symbol: 🜔 (Nitre)</note>. Pour it over one pound of Hungarian <term>vitriol</term> <note>original: ♀ hungarici; copper sulfate</note> that has been <term>calcined</term> <note>heated to a high temperature to remove moisture</note> until it reaches <term>whiteness</term> <note>original: ad albedinem</note>. Place it into a <term>retort</term> <note>a glass vessel with a long, downward-curved neck used for distillation</note> and <term>distill</term> <note>boil and condense</note> the water from it using slow degrees of heat, in such a way that the vitriol is not [overly] calcined, but only slowly [heated] up to the <term>third degree</term> <note>original: ad tertium uſque gradum; a specific level of intensity in alchemical furnace heat</note> in a <term>sand-bath</term> <note>original symbol: ∴; a container of sand used to distribute heat evenly around the glass vessel</note>. When the water has been distilled over, then take one pound of fresh water and pour it together with the distilled liquid back onto the vitriol in the retort. Let it all dissolve and <term>digest</term> <note>a process of gentle, continuous heating to promote chemical change</note> together for one <term>philosophical month</term> <note>original symbol: 🝮; in alchemical tradition, this usually signifies a period of forty days</note>.
