Retort, and distill the moisture <note>original: Humidum</note> from it by gentle degrees, so that the <term>antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁ (Antimonium)</note> is not calcined <note>reduced to a dry powder by heat</note>, but only gently up to the third degree in a <term>sand bath</term> <note>original symbol: ⸫</note>. When all of the <term>spirit</term> <note>original symbol: ~ (Spiritus)</note> has distilled over, then pour half a <term>pound</term> <note>original symbol: ℔ (Libra)</note> of fresh <term>spirit</term> into it, and pour it all back together onto the <term>antimony</term> in the retort.

Let it dissolve <note>original: solviren</note> and digest <note>original: digeriren; a process of gentle heating over time</note> together for one <term>philosophical day</term> <note>original symbol: ♃ (Jupiter/Thursday); used here to denote a specific 24-hour period or cycle</note>, then distill it again gently and only down to the third part, as before; in this way, your <term>antimony</term> will mostly have already become an <term>oil</term> <note>original symbol: ⚮ (Oleum)</note>. Then take the <term>spirit</term> that has risen over and add another half a <term>pound</term> of fresh <term>spirit</term> to it; pour it back onto the <term>antimony</term>, let it digest for one <term>day</term> <note>original symbol: ♃</note>, and afterwards distill it once more by very gentle degrees. Thus the <term>salt of antimony</term> <note>original symbols: ⊖ ♁ (Sal Antimonium)</note> will all rise over as a penetrating <term>spirit</term>.

But should something remain behind, contrary to expectation, one need only <term>cohobate</term> <note>original: cohobiren; the redistillation of a liquid by pouring it back over its solid residue</note> it until everything has risen over without any <term>residue</term> <note>original: Remanenz</note>; and with this, you have also well and truly prepared the <term>radical solvent</term> <note>original: Radicalmenstruum; a "root" solvent capable of dissolving substances to their core</note> for the vegetable work.

->Pro-<-

# Third Process.

## On the Preparation of the Animal Radical Solvent.

Recipe: Take as much <term>urine</term> <note>original: Urina; symbol: $\square$</note> from a healthy human as you wish; place it in a flask with a helm <note>the "head" or cap of a still</note> and a receiver, well sealed. Place it in a <term>water bath</term> <note>original: Balneum Mariae; a vessel of water used to heat another container gently</note> of the first degree <note>the lowest heat setting</note>, and let it stand and <term>putrefy</term> <note>the process of allowing organic matter to decay or ferment to release its "spirit"</note> for 14 <term>philosophical months</term> <note>original symbol: ♌ (Leo); alchemical "months" often refer to specific cycles of 40 days rather than calendar months</note>. Then, distill everything that will come over from the water bath by degrees; save that liquid.

Now take out the flask, put the remaining matter into a <term>retort</term> <note>a glass bulb with a long neck used for distilling liquids</note> in a <term>sand bath</term> <note>original symbol: $\therefore$</note> and drive it off once more by slow degrees. First, a <term>phlegm</term> <note>original: Phlegma; the tasteless, watery part of a distillate</note> comes forth; this is followed by a very sharp, tongue-piercing <term>spirit</term> <note>original symbol: 🜗</note>, which is the <term>animal salt</term> <note>original: Sal animale; symbol: 🜔; likely referring to ammonium carbonate</note>. This is followed by a stinking, thick <term>oil</term> <note>original: Oleum crassum; symbol: $\circ^{\circ\circ}$</note>; after this, a matter burned to coals remains at the bottom <note>original: in Fundo</note>, which is the <term>alkaline</term> part.

Take out this matter that was burned to coals and grind the thick <term>oil</term> into it. Let it <term>digest</term> <note>original: digeriren; to heat a substance gently over a long period to mature it</note> in a <term>water bath</term> for one <term>philosophical month</term>. Then pour the <term>animal salt</term> with the <term>phlegm</term> into it as well; let it digest again for one <term>philosophical month</term>, and distill it down to the third part. Pour what has come over back in once more,

->Note.<-

Here you now have the four **Main Solvents** <note>original: Hauptmenstrua</note> together, each of which has its three principles <note>Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury</note> radically unlocked within itself; from which, with which, and through which the **Mineral, Vegetable, and Universal Stone** <note>three types of the Philosopher's Stone believed to transform different kingdoms of nature</note> can be prepared in the "wet way" <note>a method of alchemy using liquid solutions and slow digestion rather than high-heat smelting</note>. And although these four waters appear very different externally, internally they are but one; and every Brother <note>a fellow member of an alchemical or Rosicrucian order</note> should know that in every such water, the four elements stand in their harmony <note>original: Concordanz</note>, which are: **Water** <note>original symbol: ∇</note>, **Fire** <note>original symbol: Δ</note>, **Air** <note>original symbol: A</note>, and **Earth** <note>original symbol: 🜃</note>. But there are also three, as **Spirit, Body, and Soul** <note>original: Spiritus, Corpus, Anima</note>; likewise **Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury** <note>original symbols: ⊖, 🜍, ☿</note>. Also **Alkali, Acid, and Volatile**. And these are also two, as **Man and Woman**, the **Agent and the Patient** <note>the active and passive forces of nature</note>, **Sun and Moon** <note>original symbols: ☉ and ☽, representing gold and silver or the fixed and the volatile</note>, which give birth to, nourish, destroy, and give birth to everything again. Thus says Brother Homer in his *Golden Chain* <note>original: Frater Homerus in sua Catena aurea; a reference to the 'Aurea Catena Homeri', a highly influential 18th-century alchemical text describing the ladder of nature</note>.

# Instruction of the Great Mystery
<note>original Latin: Instructio Mysterii Magni</note>
## Description,
### That is: The Preparation of the Mineral Stone in the Dry Way. From the Philosophical Discipline with the Agreement of the Brothers of the Golden and Rosy Cross.
<note>original Latin: Hoc est : Lapidis Mineralis Praeparatio in Via sicca. Ex Philosophica Disciplina cum Concordia Fratrum Roseae Aureae Crucis.</note>

### On the preparation of living Quicksilver.
<note>original: ☿ vivi Mercurii</note>

Place the mixture into a <term>luted retort</term> <note>original symbol: ⚗; a glass distillation vessel coated with a protective layer of clay or paste to withstand high heat</note>, which must be large enough that only a fourth of it is filled with the mass. Place it in a suitable distillation furnace that does not use a <term>sand bath</term> <note>original: Sandcapelle; a container of sand used to distribute heat evenly</note>, but is nicely rounded so that the retort fits well inside. Seal the joints with <term>lute</term> <note>original: Laimen; a specialized alchemical clay or sealant</note> so that no flames can lick around the glass. ## The Sixth Labor.

**14.** Take a drop of this <term>oil</term> <note>original symbol: 🝔</note>, which at this degree of <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: 🜂</note> should become fixed; let it fall onto a red-hot <term>copper</term> <note>original symbol: ♀</note> plate, and if it penetrates and tints this <term>copper</term> without smoking, your work is perfect.

# Corrections.

P. 160. L. 15. — — **leaves** <note>original: "Blätter"; referring to the leaves or pages of the manuscript.</note>; instead of **pustule** <note>original: "Blatter"; in German, omitting the umlaut over the 'a' accidentally changes the word for "pages" into the word for "blisters" or "smallpox."</note>.

P. 180. L. 11. — — **constellation**; instead of **constillation**.
